# GEODE Research Milestone Results Ledger

Updated: 2026-07-28

This ledger records what changed at each milestone, the evidence produced, and
which numerical differences can legitimately be interpreted as improvements.
Saved JSONL artifacts remain the machine-readable source of truth.

## Reporting rules

- Compare methods only when dataset, split hash, feature hash, and seed match.
- Report multi-seed mean and standard deviation when available.
- Treat test metrics as observational. They are never used for gradients, early
  stopping, sampling, model selection, or hyperparameter selection.
- Distinguish correctness/performance engineering from predictive improvement.
- Do not compare numbers across milestones when the validation protocol changed
  without explicitly identifying that protocol change.
- Use the final research-plan milestone IDs below. Early commit messages used
  provisional numbering and are historical provenance, not the ledger index.
- v13 milestones (M77 onward) carry a one-line summary here for continuity.
  The authoritative v13 record, including registration amendments and void
  runs, is `analysis/MILESTONE_LEDGER_v13.md`.

## Milestone summary

| Milestone                      | Main intervention                                                                                                              | Primary evidence                                                    | Measured effect                                                                                                                                                   |
| ------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Tier 6 repair baseline         | Causal temporal state, adaptive geometry, held-out calibration, corrected GPU semantics                                        | Real WikiText-103 run; full pipeline benchmark                      | 23.91% isolated and 23.35% pipeline top-1 versus 19.22% unigram in the isolated run; linear control 20.97%                                                        |
| M0: protocols and provenance   | Canonical configs, hashes, manifests, model/resource counts                                                                    | `logs/results/tier4_smoke_runs.jsonl`                               | No causal accuracy claim; complete reproducible Tier 4 artifact established                                                                                       |
| M1: semantics and determinism  | Owned RNG, fixed pruning denominator, hard-CSG gradient, CPU/GPU parity                                                        | 28 then 34 fast tests; synthetic constructor benchmark              | Correctness milestone; no predictive-improvement claim. Constructor smoke: 120 samples, 4D, 4 experts, 6 primitives in 0.078 s                                    |
| M2: shared evaluation/readouts | Disjoint geometry/calibration split and five readouts                                                                          | Tier 4 seed-42 artifact `8d40adc6757e9daf`                          | Raw to temperature: accuracy unchanged at 84.69%, NLL 1.48 to 0.50, ECE 0.60 to 0.03. Raw to multinomial: +0.19 pp accuracy, NLL 1.48 to 0.49                     |
| M3: matched baselines          | Eight baselines on identical transformed features and frozen split hashes                                                      | Five Tier 4 seeds `[11,23,37,53,71]`                                | Logistic regression 84.02% +/- 0.33; RBF SVM 84.00% +/- 0.57; GEODE multinomial 83.11% +/- 0.74. GEODE trails logistic by 0.91 pp on this protocol                |
| M4: CSG causal ablations       | A0 additive, A1 held-out excision, A2 active repair                                                                            | Five Tier 4 seeds `[11,23,37,53,71]` at 300 RANSAC iterations       | No demonstrated aggregate benefit. A1 raw: -0.0125 pp; multinomial: +0.0125 pp; paired 95% intervals include zero. A2 added no candidates                         |
| M5: high-dimensional fitting   | Synthetic robustness/budget sweeps, Tier 4 screen, locked Tier 5 confirmation                                                  | Seven synthetic fitters; five-seed Tier 4 screen; one Tier 5 seed   | Retain current fitter. Shrinkage gained +0.75 pp on Tier 4 selection but lost -1.56 pp on Tier 5 selection, used 4.4x primitives, and took 3.2x longer            |
| M6: temporal representations   | Exact, reservoir, ensemble, and hybrid causal representations                                                                  | Synthetic gates, train-only tuning, locked 50k/10k WikiText run     | Flexibility demonstration, not competitive temporal learning. GEODE 30.36%, linear 34.64%, matched 5-gram 44.50%, practical 5-gram 47.61%                         |
| M7: OOD and calibration        | Shift, selective prediction, calibration, and conformal metrics                                                                | Controlled shift; balanced CIFAR-100 and SVHN with one frozen fit   | Complete. Maximum-probability AUROC 0.786/0.831 beats Mahalanobis and k-NN on both real families, but FPR95 0.579/0.431 limits the claim to relative ranking      |
| M8: robustness and editability | Corruption, model editing, online updates                                                                                      | Five-seed robustness/stability studies and behavioral edit gate     | Complete. Broad robustness rejected; predictions are 93.0% seed-agreeing despite primitive drift; insertion/deletion/rollback are local and reversible            |
| M9: editability scaling        | One-factor class, dimension, and primitive-count sweeps                                                                        | Five-repeat locked synthetic study with operation counters          | Complete. All locality gates passed; insertion stayed below 0.18 ms, while exhaustive inference and dense 128D snapshots exposed bounded scaling costs            |
| M10: calibrated open set       | Support profiles, abstention, leave-class-out selection                                                                        | Contracts and locked three-episode toy matrix                       | Experimental only. No score/threshold/geometry cell passed 90% known coverage and 50% unknown recall across all episodes                                          |
| M11: unlabeled-group discovery | Bounded evidence, automatic clustering, stable review IDs, and gated adaptation                                                | Accumulated real-feature clustering plus GEODE action studies       | Corrected final partitions reached 61.1% distinct-group recall and full two-group recovery in 4/9 cells; semantics and mutation remained human-gated              |
| M13: primitive families        | Matched full, diagonal, and spherical fits with primitive-aware GPU CSG                                                        | Five-seed Tier 4 CPU screen and 15-cell `gfx1201` OpenCL matrix     | GPU sphere A0 reached 83.80%, +0.463 pp over diagonal; subtraction added no benefit for any family and remains disabled                                           |
| M14: probabilistic fields      | Interpret frozen covariance primitives as hierarchical Gaussian mixtures                                                       | Five-seed matched Tier 4 CPU/OpenCL semantic comparison             | Raw NLL and ECE improved strongly, but accuracy did not; sphere multinomial NLL improved 0.4847 to 0.4772 while accuracy moved -0.15 pp                           |
| M15: hybrid field readout      | Auditable calibration and matched geometric/probabilistic score fusion                                                         | Five-seed sphere study and 179-test regression gate                 | Hybrid NLL improved over both field-only inputs within the accuracy tolerance, but feature logistic remained better                                               |
| E0.1: runtime foundations      | Versioned research contracts, geometry preflight, atomic artifacts, metrics, checkpoints                                       | 19 focused runtime/legacy-manifest tests                            | Correctness and recoverability foundation only; E0 complete, E1/E2 partial, no predictive-performance claim                                                       |

| V13-I2.1    | Positively control the frozen backbone as a per-image function                                                                       | `tools/probe_backbone_batch_dependence.py`; 2 control tests                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | **Defect found.** The INT8 dinov2-small graph carries **49 `DynamicQuantizeLinear` operators** that derive activation scales from the whole input tensor, so features are a function of the _set_ of images sharing a batch. One image alone vs. inside a batch of 8 differs by `0.851` and vs. a batch of 32 by `1.210`, while reordering the same batch differs by exactly **`0.000`**. The M70 prefix control was therefore a passing detector of a defect in the old pipeline, not a failing test of the new one; it is retired in favour of a shard-invariance control. DirectML diverged 11.7% at batch 1 and was rejected. All v13 extraction runs at batch size 1 | V13-I3.1    | Audit how far the sample-adequacy and batch-dependence defects reach back through the program                                        | `tools/trace_backbone_lineage.py`; `analysis/MILESTONE_LEDGER_v13.md`                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | Exposure decided from recorded lineage, not recollection. **Defect A voids only v12**: v9 M53, v10 M58 and v11 M63/M65 fit on **800 samples per class** (`geometry_fraction 0.8` of 1000, cross-checked against the recorded `score_calibration` count of 1600), giving 25--100 samples per fitted dimension against a floor of 10, so those negatives stand; v12 M70--M73 sit at 3.125 and M74 at 1.875 and are void. **Defect B voids nothing**: it reaches v5 m19 native dinov2 (`extraction_batch_size: 32`) forward through v6, v9, v10 and v11, but the perturbation is deterministic and identical for every method on a given array, so same-array comparisons stay fair. Tier1 SDF sphere/ellipsoid, tier2 ModelNet10, tier5/tier6 MobileNetV2 (float32) and wikitext103 are exposed to neither | V13-I4.1    | Rebuild the DomainNet corpus above the M78 sample-adequacy floor                                                                     | `experiments/tier4/prepare_v13_domainnet_large.py`; `logs/results/v13/domainnet_large/`; 10 focused tests                                                                                                                                                                             | 128 classes x **576** samples = 73,728 features at 384 dimensions, extracted at **batch size 1**. The 640 registration failed closed at 81,809/81,920 because the >=256 px filter yield is not uniform (classes 65/53/96 hold only 579/603/627), so 579 is the true ceiling and 576 was registered. Geometry budget 536 per class = **16.75 samples per fitted dimension at rank 32** (floor 10), but only 8.93 at rank 60, so **rank is capped at 53**. Shard-invariance control passes at exactly **`0.000`** (1 worker vs. 4); M70 divergence is reported and never gated at 17.80% mean relative, non-zero by construction. Sharded batch-1 extraction sustained **92.4 img/s**, beating the old single-process batch-32 path at 60.2 img/s | V13-N1.1    | Test whether unseen classes can be detected at or above the freely composable bar                                                    | `tools/probe_c3_detection.py`; `tools/probe_c3_scale.py`; `logs/results/v13/c3_probe.json`                                                                                                                                                                                            | **Negative, and it voids no method.** With the instrument validated at both ends (far-field noise **1.0000**, held-out knowns **0.5136**), detection of 28 wholly held-out classes against 100 known is near chance for _everything_ on identical features: nearest class mean **0.5388**, kNN **0.5824**, geometric rank 32 **0.5898** (single-domain 0.6141/0.6228/0.6517). The freely composable controls fail alongside the geometric head, which is marginally the best of the three, so the failure is **representational**, not a defect of the geometry. The v10--v12 novelty AUROCs of 0.902--0.972 are **corpus-specific**: swept at their own 8-known/2-unknown width over five class draws, this corpus yields 0.6374 kNN and 0.6327 geometric, and widening 8 to 100 costs only ~0.04 AUROC. Also amends M78 (**R3**): at 536 samples per class the geometric head reaches **23.86%** recall at a matched 10% false-alarm rate against a 20.42% logistic bar and 19.86% kNN control, so the open-set deficit closes with sampling after all. Corpus defect recorded: the v13 corpus is **61% quickdraw**. Explored on `explore/v13-composability`, abandoned under a kill criterion registered before measurement, **not merged**                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| V13-M79.1   | Fix what v13 may claim a result on, before any result is measured                                                                    | `analysis/ACCEPTANCE_CRITERIA_v13.md`; `analysis/CLAIM_LEDGER_v13.md`                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | **Frame registered.** The substantive change is forced by N1: L2's gating threshold of **87.0%** unknown recall — set on 8-class CIFAR-10 and then carried across corpora as if it were a property of detectors — is **withdrawn as a cross-corpus constant**. On DomainNet no method reaches 25%, so retaining it would have closed the program on a corpus mismatch while the geometric head was beating every freely composable control. L2 stays gating but is restated against kNN and NCM measured on the identical corpus, with AUROC required alongside recall and with positive/negative instrument controls **mandatory**; every open-set number now carries `(recall, AUROC, corpus, class count, samples/fitted-dim)`. I5 becomes **primary and gating** (>=2x chance, strictly above kNN); I2 demoted to a structural check (v12 passed it at `1.14e-13` with I5 below kNN); I4 demoted from gating as structurally unavailable; L1's 1.0-point gate becomes a 3.0-point reported tolerance; MLP+SHAP and MLP+IG registered as controls. Two v12 gaps closed: a **deployment context** (assisted triage, 30 s / 10 active atoms) so the L1 tolerance is derived rather than asserted, blocking every accuracy-for-interpretability trade until `p(caught \| explanation)` is measured; and an **outcome taxonomy**, since no surviving document defines the A--E letters prior versions closed at. Outcome **C (frontier without dominance) is registered as a success**. Prior-art audit leaves the conjunction **narrowed, not displaced** — M80/M82 claim no methodological novelty against SpLiCE and Label-free CBM. ImageNet **disqualified for novelty** (LVD-142M was seeded with ImageNet-22k/1k and contains ImageNet-1k images), permitted for nameability from M82; corpus substitution to rescue a failing gate **prohibited**                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| V13-M80.1   | Fit an overcomplete sparse dictionary over frozen features and price the sparsification                                              | `logs/results/v13/m80_sparse_dictionary/`; `experiments/tier4/eval_v13_m80_sparse_dictionary.py`; 9 tests                                                                                                                                                                             | **H80 gate passed as registered, on a cell its own control invalidates.** Over a 3x3 grid (`m` in 2048/4096/8192, `k` in 16/32/64) at 512 fit and 64 evaluation samples per class across 128 classes, the best eligible cell is m=8192/k=64 at a **0.10-point** deficit against the 61.304% raw-feature probe, well inside the 3.0-point tolerance. **A random unit-norm dictionary of identical shape reaches 61.230% on the same probe — 0.02 points better than the trained one** — so at k=64 the probe reads a sparse random projection, not a learned decomposition. The dissociation is diagnostic: the random control's held-out R2 is **negative** (`-0.66` at m=8192) while its probe accuracy ties. Sparsity trades against learnedness monotonically: at m=8192 the deficit falls 2.99 -> 0.51 -> 0.10 as the margin over the null falls +8.68 -> +3.77 -> **-0.02**. Registration note **N80.2** records that the gate selects on accuracy alone and would have certified a random projection; the gate is not amended, but only cells beating their own null are admissible, and **M81 carries m=8192/k=32** (deficit 0.51 pt, margin +3.77 pt). Monosemanticity remains **unestablished**: the added entropy operand discriminates against its shuffled control in every cell but is estimated from ~16 activations per atom, capping observable entropy near 4 bits against a 7-bit uniform bound, so it is comparative only. Determinism defect found and contained — 8-thread fits are **not** reproducible (25.1 s/epoch single-threaded vs 8.0 s, 3.1x), so gated evidence runs single-threaded workers in parallel processes                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| V13-M81.1   | Fit heads over the M80 atoms and run the decisive forward-simulation measurement                                                     | `logs/results/v13/m81_sparse_head/`; `experiments/tier4/eval_v13_m81_sparse_head.py`; 15 tests                                                                                                                                                                                        | **H81 gate not passed; conjunction verdict `task_width_artifact`; dominance claim blocked.** Under Amendment R4 the verdict is a conjunction over two task widths. **At 8-way** (classes frozen under seed 8101) the sparse head leads the dense controls but does **not** clear its bar once read per arm: the **I5 40.22%** headline is a per-seed best-arm maximum (budget*256, budget_512, budget_512) that **N82.7 later corrected**, and the arms' own means are **38.66%** (budget_256, 5.7 cited atoms, 84.83% accuracy) and **38.21%** (budget_512), both under the 40.00% bar; the citation count that belongs with the 40.22% figure is the gate's own `unconstrained_cited_atoms_mean` of **7.51**, not 5.7. Against kNN **26.37%**, the best dense explanation (MLP+IG/EG) **35.14%**, and a shuffled-explanation null of **12.03%** — the first v13 arm to beat a dense control on the primary axis, inside the M79 10-atom budget, for 4.7 accuracy points. **At the corpus's own 128-way width no atom arm is admissible in any seed**: every arm meeting the 10-atom budget is 22-44 accuracy points below kNN's 66.13% (budget*1024 43.90%, budget_512 35.27%, budget_256 21.94%), every accuracy-competitive arm cites 32 atoms, and the dense **RBF control leads all atom arms at I5 9.42%** regardless. The failure holds at every comparability tolerance from 2.5 to 15 points. **The gate first returned `confirmed`** on `decision_list`, which has **15.74%** accuracy, emits only **36 of 128** classes, and carries a majority baseline of **2.77%** against every other arm's 0.78% — the probe was reading a collapsed prediction marginal. Two conditions stated in H81 itself were missing and are now registered: **N81.7** accuracy comparability ("a dense head \_of comparable accuracy*") and **N81.8** a prediction-collapse screen. The 8-way "confirms" is **marginal, and N82.7 later showed it does not survive at all**: the selected-maximum mean of 40.22% cleared the 40.00% bar by 0.22 points against a **3.93-point seed spread** with one seed of three clearing it, and the margin existed \_only* by per-seed arm selection — no single arm's mean reaches the bar. Under the v12 rule alone this would have read as a clear win against GEODE 17.737/RBF 22.772/kNN 25.246 — **R4 did exactly the job it was written for**. Four construction defects pinned by regression tests: **N81.3** unstandardised explanations left lbfgs unconverged so I5 measured optimiser failure; **N81.4** L1 in the loss under Adam never zeroed a coefficient (accuracy collapsed while citations stayed at 30) and was replaced by a proximal soft-threshold; **N81.5** magnitude-ranked atom budgets selected rare atoms and cited 0.6 atoms per decision; an absolute metric-field variance floor gave precision 1e6 to unseen atoms and pinned the head at exact chance. Head validated against M80's independent probe at **0.6465 vs 0.6436**. **N81.1** the SHAP arm is expected gradients, not KernelSHAP (`shap` uninstallable in the frozen venv); **N81.2** identity-withheld I5 cannot test nameability, which remains M82's burden                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| V13-M82.1   | Name the M80 atoms by caption retrieval over exemplars and test whether naming raises explanation usefulness                         | `logs/results/v13/clip_embeddings/`; `logs/results/v13/naming_vocabulary/`; `logs/results/v13/m82_atom_naming/`; `experiments/tier4/eval_v13_m82_atom_naming.py`; `experiments/common/v13_naming.py`; 20 tests                                                                        | **H82 refuted; verdict `names_unstable`; N81.2's nameability burden discharged UNMET.** Refuted on R9's own primary operand, with the instrument validated at both ends first. **The gating positive control passes decisively**: on the ~520 atoms per seed whose exemplars are ≥75% one class (6.3% of the dictionary, mean purity 0.901), CLIP recovers the ground-truth class name **89.54%** of the time (0.8865/0.9079/0.8919) against a **0.19%** shuffled-name null — so the negative is a property of the atoms, not of the naming channel. **The atoms have no stable names**: names computed from two disjoint halves of an atom's exemplars agree only **84.81%** of the time, _below_ the R6 matched-size null of **99.76%**, margin **−0.1495** on every seed (−0.1466/−0.1479/−0.1538) against a 0.0072 spread. The mechanism is visible in the channel: all 8,192 atoms are live and named, but **82% (6,704) receive a rendering-style term** rather than an object, and only **187 distinct names** cover the whole dictionary, so a randomly assembled exemplar set lands on the corpus's modal name — the corpus is 61% quickdraw — and agrees with itself almost perfectly. The channel is largely reading the corpus's marginal image distribution, not the atoms. **R8's three-arm decomposition is the result that most justifies the pre-registration**: at `sparse_linear_budget_256`, I5 is **38.66%** with identity withheld (M81's protocol, reproduced to four decimals), **90.54%** with identity revealed under an _arbitrary_ matched-size grouping (N82.4's null), and **89.83%** revealed and _named_. The naming delta is therefore **−0.71 points** (spread 0.72), and the 51.9-point gain is **identity revelation**; reported against the withheld baseline alone this would have been published as a 51.9-point naming win. Per-atom identity does better still (**93.30%**), so grouping by name _loses_ information and the name recovers none of it. `budget_512` agrees: −0.73 naming, +49.61 revelation. **Per-domain, required by N82.1**: purity-control accuracy is painting 100.00%, real 100.00%, clipart 96.72%, infograph 89.33%, **quickdraw 56.13%** (no eligible atoms in sketch, n≤2) — the instrument is weakest exactly where the corpus is densest. Far-field naming is **85.86%** raw but **22.18%** over object-named atoms only (N82.5), _below_ N82.2's 34.57% single-image floor, confirming that averaging 32 exemplars denoises substantially. **N81.7 comparability holds and M81 reproduces exactly** — head accuracy 84.83%, 5.72 cited atoms, identical per-seed I5 and nulls. Three reporting defects found and registered: **N82.5** far-field conflated style names with absent objects (the 85.86% figure was ~entirely style-named atoms), **N82.6** `dead_atom_fraction` is wrong in M80's and M81's sealed evidence (`active_atom_count()` counts atoms per row, not rows per atom; the true M82 value is **0.0000**), **N82.7** M81's 40.22% was a per-seed best-arm maximum misattributed to one arm whose own mean is 38.66% and sits _below_ the bar. Three seeds, 16.6 min, `evidence_hash 5d484f76…82bc3fc`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| V13-M83.1   | Test whether boundary supervision in absolute units produces a boundary that rejects unseen classes                                  | `logs/results/v13/m83_boundary/`; `experiments/tier4/eval_v13_m83_boundary.py`; `experiments/common/v13_boundary.py`; `experiments/configs/v13/m83_boundary.json`; 38 tests                                                                                                           | **VOID, not negative (N83.8) — corrected after this run had been sealed and written up as a refutation.** The synthetic negatives were placed **between the class centroid and the data**: the farthest probe reaches **8.61** from its owning centroid while real rows sit at a median **30.19** with their nearest decile at **24.36**. No boundary can accept rows at 24–30 and reject points at 5–8 along the same rays, so the supervision was given an incoherent problem rather than a hard one, and no rejection figure from this run is an operand. The cause is N83.6's placement unit: the median fitted tangent scale (**2.87**) is a per-direction spread inside a rank-51 subspace of 384 dimensions, while distance to centroid is dominated by the **333 residual dimensions that subspace does not describe** — the milestone never compared the two. **The tell was in the sealed evidence and was misread:** `held_out_family_rejection` is **0.0 for all four arms including `untrained`**, which performs no training at all; an operand that is identically zero for an arm that cannot have learned anything is measuring the instrument. An audit confirms the run is otherwise arithmetically sound — no NaN, no infinity, no non-finite radius, unseen minimum-scores genuinely topping out at 0.99014 — so this is a design failure, not a numerical one. **What survives:** the degeneracy instrument, which separates the two placement rules by fifteen orders of magnitude (v12-form gradient `5.42e-18` and rescale spread **exactly `0.000`**; absolute arm `2.39e-02` and `2.770`) and is independent of placement; and **N83.7**, the more consequential finding — the M80/M81 positional split takes rows 512–576 of a class-major corpus that stores all 350 quickdraw rows first, yielding a held-out set that is **0% quickdraw** against a 61%-quickdraw out-of-set, so it measured domain rather than novelty. The domain-quota partition that replaces it matches the two profiles at a deviation of **exactly 0.0** and makes the fit set representative (quickdraw 68.4% → **60.74%** against a corpus share of 60.76%). **N83.7a** records that M80 and M81 inherit that defect; their sealed evidence is not retouched. **Not claimable:** that absolute-unit supervision was tested and failed. Corrections applied for M83.2 — placement unit becomes the data extent, registered in config rather than hardcoded, and `probe_validity` becomes **gating ahead of the degeneracy contract**, returning `probe_ladder_interior` and suppressing every figure below it                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| V13-M83.2   | Re-run M83 with the probe ladder corrected, and test whether absolute-unit supervision rejects unseen classes                        | `logs/results/v13/m83_boundary_v2/`; `experiments/tier4/eval_v13_m83_boundary.py`; `experiments/common/v13_boundary.py`; `experiments/configs/v13/m83_boundary.json`; 38 tests                                                                                                        | **H83 refuted — this time on a valid instrument. Verdict `not_separable_from_null`; N83.1's improvement clause discharged UNMET.** Both premises are checked before any operand is read. The ladder now reaches past the data (farthest probe **90.49** against a known 10th percentile of **24.37**, **85.2%** of probes beyond the known median) and, decisively, held-out probe rejection is **no longer identically zero**: 0.3926 absolute, 0.4456 null, 0.4326 untrained, 0.4261 v12. The degeneracy contract separates the placement rules more sharply than before (v12-form gradient `5.42e-18`, spread **exactly `0.000`**; absolute `2.52e-01`, spread **29.12**). **The result is unchanged and now means something.** At matched 90% coverage — control accepted at **0.9178** against a 0.85 floor — out-of-set recall is **0.0000** for `m83_absolute` against **0.1226** untrained, **0.1205** null, **0.1231** v12; margins **−0.1226** and **−0.1205**. **The new fact that M83.1 could not have produced: the trained boundary can reject synthetic probes and is still worse at it than doing nothing** — 0.3926 against its own untrained initialisation's 0.4326 and the shuffled null's 0.4456 — while its real-novelty rejection collapses to zero and the null's does not. Probe-rejection skill and novelty-rejection skill are not merely unrelated here, they move in opposite directions, so the auxiliary term is actively harmful rather than inert. Mechanism: probes start far outside (mean probe score **19.41**, active fraction 0.0001) and training drags them onto the boundary (**1.73**, active 0.334) while the known term falls 7.76 → 0.0596; the absolute arm moves _less_ than the null (shape 0.4323 vs 0.5768) and rejects less on both operands. **Why it cannot work is now the finding:** real out-of-set images are not radially distinguishable from known ones on this corpus — within each domain, known and unseen rows sit at near-identical distance from the global mean (44.15/43.81, 46.09/46.10, 50.42/50.42, 53.76/54.00) — so no radius-parameterised boundary separates them, and synthetic negatives placed radially outward supervise a direction along which novelty does not live. Three seeds, absolute-arm spread **exactly 0.0**, `sealed: true`, 28.8 min, evidence `47c513ad…`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| V13-M84     | Test whether exposure to real out-group images, scaled along count and diversity, produces a boundary that rejects unseen classes    | `logs/results/v13/m84_exposure_ladder/`; `logs/results/v13/exposure/`; `experiments/tier4/eval_v13_m84_exposure_ladder.py`; `experiments/common/v13_boundary.py`; `experiments/configs/v13/m84_exposure_ladder.json`; 48 tests                                                        | **H84 refuted; verdict `ladder_flat`; sealed. The open-set leg is closed for this parameterisation.** Every premise passes before an operand is read: exposure-term activity **0.90** against N84.6's 0.5 floor (it reads **0.0000** at the raw fitted radii, which is why N84.6 moved the initialisation), the zero rung reproduces N84.4's pre-registered **0.11875 exactly on all three seeds**, and N83.3's known-class control accepts **0.9107** (minimum 0.8975) against a 0.85 floor. **Exposure does not merely fail to help, it destroys rejection.** Against a 0.11875 untrained baseline, the exposure arm scores **0.00006 / 0.00000 / 0.00012 / 0.00000 / 0.00000 / 0.00000 / 0.00000 / 0.00000** across the eight feasible rungs from `N_out`=10 to 10,000; the best cell in the whole ladder is **0.000116** at `n10_d10`, three orders of magnitude below doing nothing. **N84.3's moment-matched null beats real exposure at every single rung** (0.0776 / 0.0142 / 0.0315 / 0.0134 / 0.0030 / 0.0777 / 0.0366 / 0.0563), so the structure of real out-group images is worth strictly less than a Gaussian carrying only their first two moments. There is no monotone trend in either count or diversity. **N84.5's prediction is confirmed as mechanism and refuted as benefit**: the response is exactly the predicted tangent anisotropy rather than a smaller mean radius, and it scales cleanly with exposure — 0.4344 untrained → 0.4981, 0.6373, 0.5047, 0.7513, 1.2006, 0.8168, 1.3966, **1.5201** at `N_out`=10,000 — while rejection sits at zero throughout. The boundary moves, a great deal, in the predicted direction, and every unit of that movement costs detection. **The mechanism is visible in two diagnostics.** First, `owner_agreement` is **≤ 0.0003 across all 48 trained arms**: essentially every negative escapes the class it was assigned to and is immediately re-absorbed by a different one. Rejection requires the minimum score over all 128 classes to exceed 1, but the objective only ever pushes a point out of one of them, so ejection is whack-a-mole against 127 remaining ellipsoids. Second, `known_false_rejection` falls to **exactly 0.0** on every exposure arm while `known_novel_acceptance` holds at ~0.913 — each class boundary still contains exactly its coverage-matched 90%, yet the **union** of 128 elongated ellipsoids now covers the space. A boundary can be per-class coverage-correct and useless as a detector at the same time, and anisotropy is how it gets there. **The decomposition that reframes the whole leg**: the 0.11875 baseline is not weak-everywhere, it is an average over domains with wildly different power. Untrained unseen-vs-known rejection reads **0.733 vs 0.162 on real photographs**, 0.433 vs 0.195 on painting, 0.181 vs 0.074 on infograph, 0.169 vs 0.031 on clipart — and **0.0003 vs 0.0000 on quickdraw**, which is 3,510 of the 5,760 evaluation rows because N83.2 matches the out-of-set to a corpus that is 61% quickdraw. The geometry detects novelty perfectly respectably wherever the images are photographic, and has no power at all on the domain that dominates the corpus by its own matching rule. `evidence_hash 68015109b3ab8b66ff7d4eb496c31a837869cfcdaf3cb125ed765fa7ca27cdf5`; 27 fits, 3 seeds, 62.7 min. **N84.2 was amended before the run** — `(N_out=10, d=100)` is infeasible in arithmetic, not in DomainNet, so the ladder is eight cells plus the zero rung and the diversity axis is complete only at `N_out` ≥ 100. |

Nothing is re-trained: the run gates on reproducing M84's zero rung, measuring **0.11875 against the registered 0.11875**, so the ranked object is provably the thresholded object. The instrument is validated at both ends for all three scorers — far-field control **1.0000** against a 0.99 floor, held-out-known split **0.4828-0.4845** against +/-0.02 about chance. **The fitted geometry reads AUROC 0.5851 and does not clear its own free baseline**: distance to the 10th nearest frozen fit row reads **0.5749** and distance to the nearest class centre **0.5617**, so the margin bought by Phase A and Phase B is **0.0102**, inside the 0.02 decisive margin registered before the run. Two findings follow. **The pooled figure sits below every one of its parts** — 0.7092 clipart, 0.5841 infograph, 0.6759 painting, 0.6404 quickdraw, 0.8691 real, weighting to 0.6580 within domain for a Simpson gap of **+0.0729** that appears in both free baselines too, because the score separates rendering style more strongly than novelty. **Ranking and thresholding come apart entirely**: quickdraw ranks at 0.6404 while its rejection recall is **0.0003**, and real photographs rank at 0.8691 with recall **0.7333**. Per N85.4a, registered before the numbers were seen, a threshold-free result cannot overturn a threshold result: H83 and H84 stay refuted, and the coverage-matching finding is a diagnostic needing new registration.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |

Resolution costs **+0.0876**; **the corpus costs -0.0097 beyond it**, inside the 0.05 margin registered before the run, so the corpus effect is a tie and CIFAR-100 in fact retains marginally better than the corpus's own images at the same resolution. | V13-M87     | Audit the five claims v13 would put in a write-up against the literature, before writing it up                                       | `analysis/PRIOR_ART_AUDIT_v13.md` (Sections 1-7); `logs/results/v13/m87_prior_art_audit/`; `experiments/tier4/audit_v13_prior_art.py`; `experiments/configs/v13/m87_prior_art_audit.json`; 12 tests                                                                                   | **The claim v13 would have led with does not survive its own audit. Nothing in v13 moved (N87.1); what moved is the contribution attached to two results.** Five claims, 37 queries, 627 records over arXiv and Semantic Scholar. **C2 - that domain-pooled OOD AUROC conceals within-domain performance because the score separates domain rather than novelty - is narrowed heavily and barred from being presented as a contribution.** Yang, Zhou & Liu, _Full-Spectrum Out-of-Distribution Detection_, arXiv:2204.05306, is built around that exact confound: it separates covariate-shifted ID from near- and far-OOD across three benchmarks and proposes a score whose stated purpose is that the non-semantic part cancels. M85a measured the gap on a six-style corpus; it did not discover it. Simpson-type reversals in ML evaluation are separately established in recsys and spatio-temporal validation, so the statistics were not new either. C1 survives only as _unresolved_, C4 and C5 fall to positioning. **The instrument is the other finding, and it nearly produced the opposite conclusion.** Three defects were caught before the audited run (N87.9); the one that mattered is that arXiv's exact-phrase syntax returned zero hits for every query **including all three planted anchors** - without anchors that would have yielded a clean sweep of _unresolved_ verdicts and the reading that everything was novel. The repaired instrument re-ran every query, not only the empty ones. **Semantic Scholar refused 26 of 37 queries with HTTP 429 after retry**, including all six of C3's, so C3 was searched on one index. C5 returned **none** of the forward-simulation literature it was aimed at - a measured demonstration of under-return rather than a hypothetical caveat - so every _unresolved_ verdict is discounted and none licenses the word first. Outcome C stands exactly as sealed.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| V13-M88     | Discharge the three obligations M87 wrote against itself, and measure whether the search instrument can retrieve work known to exist | `analysis/PRIOR_ART_AUDIT_v13.md` (Section 8); `logs/results/v13/m88_prior_art_recheck/`; `experiments/tier4/audit_v13_prior_art.py`; `experiments/common/test_v13_prior_art_recheck.py`; `experiments/configs/v13/m88_prior_art_recheck.json`; `tools/build_m88_config.py`; 13 tests | **All three obligations discharged; all three reopened claims fall; the run exits non-zero by design because its own positive control failed.** Registered in full (N88.1-N88.6) at commit `bc44355` **before** any code was written or any query run, because a second search is the easiest place to quietly relitigate an unwelcome result: scope closed to M87's obligations, C2's demotion final, a third index able only to find and never to establish absence (N88.3), and verdicts permitted to move **down only** (N88.4). **The headline is that M87's health check could not have detected the problem.** All three indexes were live and all three anchors returned work - 60 arXiv, 60 OpenAlex, 20 Semantic Scholar, `instrument_live: true` - which on M87's evidence is the entire check, and it passes. The registered recall probes, seven named papers each paired with a topic query that omits its title, retrieved **4 of 7**: P2 _Exposing Outlier Exposure_, P4 _Sanity Checks for Saliency Maps_ and P6 _Full-Spectrum OOD_ were each missed across ~40 records from two working indexes. **The positive control failed and the registered interpretation of that failure was wrong.** P6 was registered on the reasoning that M87 had already found the paper, so a miss meant broken code; the runner still prints that sentence and it is left in place and contradicted rather than edited. The code is not broken - 25 tests pass, including one asserting a probe is not satisfied by an adjacent paper and one asserting no probe query leaks its own title, and the same matching path succeeded four times. The only difference between finding and missing arXiv:2204.05306 is the phrasing of the query. **Retrieval is a lottery over query phrasing**, so C1, C2 and C5 drop to _not searched_ under N88.2. **The sharpest result is C3's:** its six registered queries returned **231 distinct records across three indexes and contained no trace of FixRes** - Touvron et al., the canonical statement that train/test resolution rather than data explains apparent accuracy loss, and the single most obviously relevant paper to the claim. It appears in the evidence once, via probe P3, a query written in advance to catch it. A family can look thoroughly searched and miss the paper a reviewer names first. **C1 is narrowed by primary text (N88.5).** Liznerski et al., arXiv:2205.11474, read in full rather than by abstract as M87 had: §5.2 finds "more OE samples are necessary when the normal class is not concentrated" on a multimodal leave-one-class-out benchmark, and Table 3 shows the worst single OE sample driving detection below chance (CIFAR-10 43.3/31.6, ImageNet-10 39.2/26.3). Both of C1's components have precedent; the union-of-128 structure remains a difference in setting, which M87 had already registered as a hypothesis. **C3 is narrowed** by Arasteh et al., arXiv:2510.07191, whose title states C3's headline outright and which finds DINOv3 beats DINOv2 at 512px but not at 224px on 816,183 radiographs. Semantic Scholar failed 25 of 30 queries including **all seven probes**, so its own recall is unmeasured while it supplied that hit. **Registered consequence: v13 makes no novelty claim of any kind and never asserts absence of prior art.** Evidence is a dated snapshot and is not replayable (N88.7).                                                                                                                                                        |
| V14-M89     | Measure the structure of the frozen representation that v13's rejection failure sits on top of                                       | `analysis/RESEARCH_IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN_v14.md`; `logs/results/v14/m89_representation_diagnostic/`; `experiments/tier4/eval_v14_m89_representation_diagnostic.py`; `experiments/configs/v14/m89_representation_diagnostic.json`; `experiments/common/test_v14_representation_diagnostic.py`; 9 tests | **Non-gating by construction (N89.1), and it locates v13's failure in the coordinates rather than in the boundary.** Identity passes first (N89.3): the recomputed geometry reproduces M84's zero-rung recall `0.11875` and M85a's AUROC `0.585085105895996` exactly, so the object described is the object M83, M84 and M85 thresholded. **Acceptance is multiply owned.** At the 90% matched coverage all three of those milestones used, a known evaluation row is interior to a mean of **78.74 of the 128 class regions**, median 114; **11.65%** are inside all 128 and only **10.94%** inside exactly one. Rejection is a conjunction over classes, so M84's 2-rejections-in-17,280 is arithmetic, not a training failure. Note the union working against the boundary: coverage is matched per class at 90%, so ~10% of rows should fall outside their own region, yet only **3.83%** fall outside every region. **Class clouds are wider than the gaps between them** - spread/separation **2.338** at one centroid per class, falling to **1.384** at one centroid per (class, domain) cell. **The spread is visual domain, not semantic class:** the median gap between a class's own domain cells is **32.295**, larger than the **19.153** median gap to the nearest foreign-class cell, so the same object in two domains is further apart than two different objects in one. Per-domain spread runs 27.14 (quickdraw) to 48.38 (real), and quickdraw is 39,808 of 65,536 fit rows, dragging every class centroid where the other five domains do not live. Features are **not normalised** (median L2 norm 46.81). The geometry still carries class signal it cannot convert into a boundary: argmin-score accuracy **0.5076** against a 0.0078 chance floor, barely above nearest-centroid's 0.4814. **A corpus limitation is recorded for the first time:** domain 5 (sketch) is 0 of 13,865 open-set rows and 158 of 73,728 known rows, a consequence of `minimum_native_short_edge: 256`; not a defect, since N83.2 requires only that known and out-of-set be domain-matched and they are, but a caveat every v13 per-domain figure carries. **Registered forward:** 216 (class, domain) cells clear support 100, at which the standing 10-samples-per-fitted-dimension floor permits a common rank of **10** against v13's 51 - M90's mixture arm buys components with rank, and its null holds both constant. Two runs byte-identical at `264bf2a145375a8c0958665f66bf1cea0359feefd9e9f3bd7d847a5dd6aa03a7`; the hash excludes `generated_at` and `runtime_seconds` so it is itself the replay check. Nothing here may revisit a v13 verdict (N89.4); Outcome C stands as sealed. |
| V14-M90     | Try the three corrections that need no training, and see whether any of them rescues v13's rejection                                  | `analysis/RESEARCH_IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN_v14.md`; `logs/results/v14/m90_representation_remedies/`; `experiments/tier4/eval_v14_m90_representation_remedies.py`; `experiments/configs/v14/m90_representation_remedies.json`; `experiments/common/test_v14_representation_remedies.py`; 15 tests | **H91 refuted, H90 undetermined, and M91 does not open.** Six arms over M89's sealed partition, each an independent refit, gated on all three of multiplicity < 40 (baseline 78.74), recall > 0.11875 (M84's zero rung) and AUROC > 0.585085 + 0.02 (M85a). **No arm clears any of the three bars.** L2 normalisation is inert - multiplicity 78.35 vs 78.74, recall **0.10955** (below baseline), AUROC 0.5864, known accuracy 0.5095; putting every row on the unit sphere does not separate what the norm was not carrying. **Per-domain centring is worse on every operand it was meant to fix**: multiplicity rises to **80.22**, recall falls to 0.11580, AUROC falls to 0.5837, accuracy falls to 0.5007. Its null - centring on random groups of the same size profile, seed 90002 - reproduces the baseline to five figures (78.75 / 0.11875 / 0.5851), which is the null behaving exactly as designed: subtracting a random group mean is near a global translation, and an ellipsoidal region is translation-equivariant, so the null has nothing to do and does nothing. Domain centring therefore had a real effect and the effect was negative. **The mixture arm is void, not negative.** 344 components (216 (class, domain) cells at support >= 100, plus one class-level component each) at rank 10 to hold 10 samples per fitted dimension, coverage matched per class over the union of its components by the conformal order statistic on the union score (N90.7). It fails its own known-split negative control at **0.47506** against a 0.48 floor, so every operand below it is suppressed (N90.8) - including the fact that its **random-cell null beat it** (recall 0.12882 vs 0.08715, AUROC 0.5953 vs 0.5890, seed 90003), which is the shape of a partition that is spending its rank budget worse than chance. An arm that fails its instrument refutes nothing (M83.1 precedent), so H90 is **undetermined** and M91 stays shut. **The control itself is defective, and the defect is inherited.** The known-split control halves the report rows by position, but those rows are class-sorted, so the halves are classes 0-64 and 64-127 with exactly one class in common - a class-block split, not a random split. The domain mixtures do match (`[284,252,185,1241,86,0]` vs `[292,196,199,1255,106,0]`), but M85's claim that it "measures the scorer rather than the split" is wrong: it measures whether a scorer scores the lower and upper halves of the class index differently. All six arms sit below 0.5, from **0.4751 to 0.4860**, a one-sided bias rather than null noise. The control is left exactly as registered and its verdict stands for this run; repairing a control after watching it fail an arm is the error this program exists to avoid. The repair is registered as **M90.1** and will be applied to every arm including the baseline, and only M90.1 can validly decide H90. **This does not void M85** - its arms passed this control, and passing a mis-aimed control does not invalidate a measurement; only the docstring's interpretive claim is withdrawn. Positive control 1.0 on all six arms, known-accuracy floor 0.99 held, replay byte-identical, evidence `350167a2a24932585590060e4614a083cbfe112865c596904d950d44c46f7f99`, 2.3 min. |
| V14-M90.1   | Repair the negative control M90 inherited, and retry the hypothesis it voided                                                          | `analysis/RESEARCH_IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN_v14.md` §6.3; `logs/results/v14/m90_1_control_repair/`; `experiments/tier4/eval_v14_m90_1_control_repair.py`; `experiments/configs/v14/m90_1_control_repair.json`; `experiments/common/test_v14_control_repair.py`; 13 tests | **H90 refuted, and with H91 already refuted at M90, M91 opens.** The known-split negative control is replaced by a random split drawn inside every (class, domain) cell, so the halves are exchangeable in both factors at once - a split stratified on class alone would still be free to skew domain, and the M90 defect was exactly a split that balanced one factor while destroying the other. **The repaired control behaves like a control.** All six arms land between **0.49524 and 0.49765**, scattered around 0.5, against **0.47506 to 0.48602** under the class-block split, which was one-sided on every single arm. The split profiles are in the evidence rather than asserted: class-block halves carry **65 and 64 classes with 1 in common**; stratified halves carry **128 and 128 with 128 in common**, and identical domain mixtures. Per N90.1.2 the repair is applied to every arm including the baseline, and per N90.1.6 both controls are reported for all six. **The operands did not move**: largest absolute delta against M90's sealed evidence is **exactly 0.0** across multiplicity, recall, AUROC, known accuracy and the positive control, checked in-run against M90's registered hash `350167a2...`, so the only difference between the two milestones is the control. **The mixture arm is now measurable and it loses.** 344 components at rank 10: multiplicity **75.87** against a bar of 40, recall **0.08715** against 0.11875, AUROC **0.5890** against 0.605085 - failing all three, with its own random-cell null ahead of it on both discriminative operands (**0.12882** and **0.5953**). A domain-aware mixture spending its rank budget on 344 components does worse than the same budget spent at random, which is the shape of a partition that is not buying what it costs. Under N90.1.4 that refutes H90. **Both untrained explanations for v13's rejection failure are now closed**: it is not misspecification of one region per class across six domains, and it is not unnormalised or un-centred coordinates. What remains is the representation itself, so §7 opens. M85 is not voided - its arms passed the old control, and passing a mis-aimed control does not invalidate a measurement; one interpretive docstring sentence is withdrawn (N90.1.5). Replay byte-identical, evidence `ce88fb2a737083765c544e1ba9cd22b4889ba5052c6f6dc1f6ecef941a026940`, 2.6 min, 212 v13+v14 tests pass. |
| V14-M90.2   | Recompute M89's domain-dominance measure under transforms drawn from the domain-generalisation literature, and ask whether removing domain helps rejection | `analysis/RESEARCH_IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN_v14.md` §6.4; `logs/results/v14/m90_2_domain_erasure/`; `experiments/tier4/eval_v14_m90_2_domain_erasure.py`; `experiments/configs/v14/m90_2_domain_erasure.json`; `experiments/common/test_v14_domain_erasure.py`; 19 tests | **H94 refuted, H95 untestable, and the shape of the refutation is the finding: domain became exactly linearly unreadable and stayed geometrically dominant.** M90 and M90.1 never recomputed M89's dominance measure — they read downstream operands only — so this milestone carries `separation_report` through six arms and adds an `erasure_certificate` that verifies the transform instead of inferring it. The baseline reproduces M90.1's operands exactly (`operands_reproduce` true against `ce88fb2a…`), so the transformed object is the object M90 measured. **LEACE (arXiv:2306.03819) achieves a perfect erasure**: largest pairwise domain-mean gap **35.2 → 1.06e-07**, cross-covariance with the one-hot domain likewise, at the analytically predicted rank of 5, driving a held-out domain probe to **0.2000 — exactly chance for the five domains present** — against a budget-matched null at 0.8875 and a baseline at 0.8946. **And the geometry barely noticed**: the sibling/foreign dominance ratio falls only **1.450 → 1.234** and stays above 1.0, so a class's own domain cells remain further apart than its nearest foreign-class cell when no linear classifier can tell the domains apart at all. Domain structure here is not confined to a five-dimensional linear subspace, so no projection removes it. Rejection is unmoved under every arm (recall 0.11684 and AUROC 0.5839 for LEACE against 0.11875 / 0.5851); H95 is recorded **untestable rather than refuted** because no arm satisfied its antecedent. ABTT (arXiv:1702.01417) removes 5 top directions and only partly erases (meangap 17.7, probe 0.7522) while its own null sits at 0.8930. **`whiten` reproduces RobustNet's stated lesson** — full ZCA drops known accuracy **0.5076 → 0.3186** and inflates multiplicity to 93.37 while the probe stays at 0.8933; whitening everything destroys content, which is why RobustNet is selective. DomainBed's caution holds: no domain-aware transform beat its own null on any rejection operand. **Six defects and four failed registered judgements are on the record rather than repaired.** The one that mattered: the corpus is float32, and a 384×384 inverse square root in float32 left a residual mean gap of **1.76e-01** — an exact closed-form guarantee degraded to a mere 200-fold reduction, which would have been written up as "LEACE only partly erases domain", a finding about a dtype masquerading as a finding about data. The certificate caught it; float64 arithmetic with a cast back to the corpus dtype fixed it. Also caught: an unconverged probe (200 lbfgs iterations on every arm), which understates the signal it measures and therefore reads as successful erasure — convergence is now part of instrument validity, and the pre-amendment figures are recorded because the results had been seen; a null removing **6** directions against its arm's 5, violating R5, because a relative tolerance has no signal to be relative to when the null is pure noise — the rank is now capped at the analytically known `g-1`; and a chance level registered as 1/6 when the report rows contain 5 domains. Withdrawn and contradicted rather than edited (M88 precedent): N90.2.4's rule that a probe above chance means LEACE was misapplied, and N90.2.14's second-moment explanation, which was explaining a float32 artefact. **M91 is unaffected** — M90.1 opened it — but it inherits a sharper question, since "domain dominance that survives complete linear erasure" is now a named property a backbone can be asked to improve. Sealed at `0120ccc080154b627343e3c7f0c9c4e0c9f78d7b6048ef0cb461371f1a928185`, 14.0 min, CPU-only. |
| V14-M91     | Ask whether a higher-capacity backbone changes the rejection result, on rows identical to v13's | `analysis/RESEARCH_IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN_v14.md` §7.1-§7.2; `logs/results/v14/m91_backbone_capacity/`; `logs/results/v14/m91_backbones/`; `experiments/tier4/eval_v14_m91_backbone_capacity.py`; `experiments/tier4/prepare_v14_m91_backbones.py`; `experiments/configs/v14/m91_backbone_capacity.json`; `experiments/configs/v14/m91_backbones.json`; `experiments/common/test_v14_backbone_capacity.py`; 20 tests | **H92 is untestable, and the two confounds found while executing it are worth more than the verdict.** dinov2-base (768-d) and dinov2-large (1024-d) were extracted on rows identical to v13's entry-for-entry (N91.2), and neither beat dinov2-small on known-class accuracy — base 0.122314 (delta -0.385), large 0.410645 (delta -0.097) against the reference 0.507568 — so under N91.5 no arm demonstrated added capacity and H92's antecedent was never satisfied. The instrument is sound: the reference arm re-extracted through the new code path reproduced v13's sealed `features_sha256` and recovered M84's recall 0.11875 and M85a's AUROC 0.585085105895996 exactly (N91.8), every arm's negative controls were valid, and every domain probe converged (404 / 262 / 193 iterations against a 2000 budget). **Confound one, N91.11:** the N91.6 quantisation control, registered as a formality, found the published INT8 graphs are damaged idiosyncratically — mean relative INT8-vs-fp32 divergence 0.3150 (small), **1.1944 (base)**, 0.4812 (large), where unrelated vectors diverge by about 1.41. base's collapse is best read as a broken checkpoint, not evidence about capacity, so N91.1's "single-factor by construction" is contradicted in place: the design is single-factor in provenance, not in fidelity. **Confound two, N91.12:** rank is pinned at 51 by the ten-samples-per-fitted-dimension floor on 512 fit rows per class, which is independent of ambient dimension — so the arms are fitted at 0.8156 / 0.6977 / 0.6902 retained within-class variance, handicapping exactly the arms under test, and it is not repairable without breaching sample adequacy or N91.8. Both confounds run against H92, so the defensible claim is the weaker one: **at 512 fit rows per class the permitted rank is 51 whatever the representation's width, so this geometry cannot exploit a wider backbone** — scale is unreachable in this sample regime, not shown useless. Rejection operands are recorded and excluded from the verdict (N91.5); dinov2-large did clear recall at 0.15625 but failed multiplicity (72.00 vs bar 40) and AUROC (0.5897 vs bar 0.6051). A second registered judgement failed: an earlier draft of N91.11 asserted quantisation damage grows with model size, a trend fitted to two arms and refuted by the third, recorded as withdrawn rather than deleted. Evaluator sealed `477837e1…`, evidence `a4aff638…`, byte-identical on replay. |

## M0: protocols and provenance

Deliverables:

- canonical experiment IDs from configuration JSON;
- git, environment, device, dataset, split, and feature fingerprints;
- model primitive/parameter/byte counts and runtime statistics;
- locked Tier 4, Tier 5, and Tier 6 configs;
- benchmark protocol manifest.

First complete Tier 4 smoke artifact:

- held-out accuracy: 84.81%;
- CV accuracy: 83.48%;
- 34 experts, 42 additive and 14 subtractive ellipsoids;
- fit time: 10.71 seconds.

This run predates the disjoint geometry/calibration protocol introduced in M2,
so it must not be used as a direct improvement comparison against M2/M3.

## M1: correctness, semantics, and determinism

Changes:

- construction and subtractive fitting use owned seeded random generators;
- normalized-softmin pruning retains total mixture count;
- hard CSG gradients follow the active hard-max branch;
- finite-difference and CPU/OpenCL parity tests cover these semantics.

Observed engineering results:

- pruning defect had an exact field shift of `log(4)/2 = 0.693147` in the
  four-expert regression and was removed;
- vectorized optimizer matched scalar loss exactly, with precision error about
  `1e-14`, and measured 37.9x synthetic speedup;
- synthetic constructor smoke completed in 0.078 seconds.

These are correctness and runtime results, not evidence of accuracy gain.

M1.6 added a research-only multistart numerical closest-point solver for 2D/3D
ellipsoids and compared it with `compute_metric_sdf` over eccentricities 1-8,
64 directions, and five inside/outside radial factors. Spheres matched exactly
to numerical tolerance. Maximum absolute error reached 3.13 units in 2D and
2.53 units in 3D at eccentricity 8. None of the sampled outside steps toward
the numerical closest point crossed the isolated primitive boundary. The
result validates the implementation as a local first-order correction only;
it does not establish exact distance or safe tracing for fused/CSG fields.

## M2: shared evaluation and readout decomposition

Tier 4 seed-42 held-out results:

| Readout          | Accuracy | Balanced accuracy |  NLL | Brier |  ECE |
| ---------------- | -------: | ----------------: | ---: | ----: | ---: |
| Raw SDF          |   84.69% |            84.58% | 1.48 |  0.65 | 0.60 |
| Temperature      |   84.69% |            84.58% | 0.50 |  0.23 | 0.03 |
| Diagonal         |   84.44% |            84.33% | 0.49 |  0.23 | 0.03 |
| Multinomial      |   84.88% |            84.75% | 0.49 |  0.23 | 0.04 |
| Feature logistic |   84.69% |            84.53% | 0.49 |  0.23 | 0.02 |

Interpretation:

- most calibration benefit comes from a scalar temperature, not a more
  expressive cross-class readout;
- multinomial calibration adds only 0.19 percentage points over raw geometry on
  this seed;
- raw SDF accuracy is competitive but its probabilities are severely
  miscalibrated.

Protocol:

- 5,122 geometry samples;
- 1,278 disjoint calibration samples;
- 1,600 untouched test samples;
- 15 records: five readouts for two CV folds and final test.

## M3: matched classical baselines

Five-seed Tier 4 final-test accuracy:

| Method                      | Mean accuracy | Standard deviation |
| --------------------------- | ------------: | -----------------: |
| Logistic regression         |        84.02% |               0.33 |
| RBF SVM                     |        84.00% |               0.57 |
| Feature logistic readout    |        83.61% |               0.25 |
| Linear SVM                  |        83.34% |               0.54 |
| Nearest centroid            |        83.19% |               0.52 |
| Shrinkage Gaussian          |        83.12% |               0.74 |
| GEODE multinomial           |        83.11% |               0.74 |
| GEODE raw                   |        82.96% |               0.57 |
| GEODE temperature           |        82.96% |               0.57 |
| GEODE diagonal              |        82.82% |               0.62 |
| Histogram gradient boosting |        82.76% |               0.42 |
| k-NN                        |        82.35% |               0.70 |
| Matched GMM                 |        81.91% |               0.82 |

All five artifacts contain 39 comparison rows and zero within-split protocol
hash mismatches. The result does not support an accuracy advantage over simple
logistic regression on the fixed MobileNetV2 representation.

Resource observations on the seed-42 smoke geometry set:

- histogram gradient boosting: 1.13 s fit;
- calibrated RBF SVM: 0.60 s fit;
- matched GMM: 0.27 s fit;
- all other methods: below 0.1 s fit.

## M4: causal CSG and calibration ablations

Protocol:

- seeds `[11, 23, 37, 53, 71]`;
- one frozen additive fit per seed;
- A0: additive only;
- A1: standard excision accepted only by a disjoint carve slice;
- A2: active repair applied cumulatively after A1;
- 300 RANSAC iterations, selected because the 10-iteration toy budget
  suppressed candidate discovery, not because of test accuracy;
- separate geometry, carve acceptance, score calibration, and untouched test
  samples;
- paired test predictions retained for bootstrap analysis.

M4.4 regenerated the same five locked seeds with the eight M3 baselines on the
exact CSG geometry and test splits. The summary now retains absolute accuracy,
NLL, ECE, timing, and complexity means for every method/readout in addition to
the paired CSG deltas.

Selected absolute means:

| Method/readout                 | Accuracy |   NLL |   ECE |
| ------------------------------ | -------: | ----: | ----: |
| RBF SVM                        |   84.21% | 0.697 | 0.058 |
| GEODE feature-logistic (A0)    |   84.03% | 0.465 | 0.021 |
| Direct logistic regression     |   83.96% | 0.456 | 0.019 |
| GEODE temperature scaling (A0) |   83.66% | 0.490 | 0.028 |
| GEODE raw (A0)                 |   83.66% | 1.509 | 0.594 |

This closes the Tier 4 baseline/readout reporting gap without changing the
negative causal conclusion below. Tier 5 still needs the same complete matrix.

Carve audit:

- A1 decisions: 31;
- accepted: 2; rejected: 29;
- accepted candidates recovered 34 carve-set false positives and damaged 2
  carve-set true positives;
- A2 decisions: 0; no active-repair primitive reached acceptance scoring.

Five-seed test deltas:

| Readout     | Mean A1-A0 | Pooled paired 95% interval | Mean A2-A1 |
| ----------- | ---------: | -------------------------: | ---------: |
| Raw         | -0.0125 pp |      [-0.0625, +0.0375] pp |  0.0000 pp |
| Temperature | -0.0125 pp |      [-0.0625, +0.0375] pp |  0.0000 pp |
| Diagonal    | +0.0125 pp |      [-0.0375, +0.0625] pp |  0.0000 pp |
| Multinomial | +0.0125 pp |      [-0.0750, +0.1000] pp |  0.0000 pp |

Held-out acceptance prevents harmful carving, but the accepted CSG primitives
do not provide a measurable aggregate Tier 4 benefit under this protocol. The
result does not support enabling subtraction by default. The A2 result means
"no candidate generated or accepted," not that an effective active-repair
carve is known to have zero effect.

## M5: high-dimensional fitter study (synthetic gate)

The first locked sweep used dimensions `[3, 6, 9, 12, 19]`, five seeds, 300
fitting samples, 2,000 clean held-out samples, anisotropy 8, and 15% combined
